src/parser.rs
┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄ :— 

use crate::ast::*;
use crate::lexer::{Kind, Kw, Lexer, P, Span, Tok};

pub struct Parser<'a> {
    src: &'a [u8],
    toks: Vec<Tok>,
    pos: usize,
    errors: Vec<ParseErr>,
}

#[derive(Debug, Clone)]
pub struct ParseErr {
    pub msg: String,
    pub span: Span,
    pub note: Option<String>,
}

pub type PResult<T> = Result<T, ParseErr>;

impl<'a> Parser<'a> {
    pub fn parse(src: &'a [u8]) -> Result<Program, Vec<ParseErr>> {
        let mut toks = Vec::new();
        Lexer::tokenize_all(src, &mut toks);

        if let Some(bad) = toks.iter().find(|t| matches!(t.kind, Kind::Err(_))) {
            let msg = match bad.kind {
                Kind::Err(crate::lexer::LexErr::UnexpectedByte(b)) => {
                    format!("unexpected byte `{}` (0x{:02x})", printable(b), b)
                }
                Kind::Err(crate::lexer::LexErr::UnterminatedStr) => {
                    "unterminated string literal".into()
                }
                Kind::Err(crate::lexer::LexErr::UnterminatedComment) => {
                    "unterminated block comment".into()
                }
                _ => "lexer error".into(),
            };
            return Err(vec![ParseErr {
                msg,
                span: bad.span,
                note: None,
            }]);
        }

        let mut p = Self {
            src,
            toks,
            pos: 0,
            errors: Vec::new(),
        };
        let mut decls = Vec::new();

        while p.is_decl_start() {
            match p.decl() {
                Ok(d) => decls.push(d),
                Err(e) => {
                    p.errors.push(e);
                    p.recover_decl();
                }
            }
        }

        let body = if matches!(p.cur().kind, Kind::Eof) {
            Box::new(Expr {
                span: Span { lo: 0, hi: 0 },
                kind: ExprKind::Int(0),
            })
        } else {
            match p.expr() {
                Ok(e) => Box::new(e),
                Err(e) => {
                    p.errors.push(e);
                    p.recover_decl();
                    Box::new(Expr {
                        span: Span { lo: 0, hi: 0 },
                        kind: ExprKind::Int(0),
                    })
                }
            }
        };

        if !matches!(p.cur().kind, Kind::Eof) {
            p.errors.push(ParseErr {
                msg: format!("expected end of input, found {}", describe(&p.cur())),
                span: p.cur().span,
                note: Some("remove the trailing tokens or wrap them in a `let` binding".into()),
            });
        }

        if p.errors.is_empty() {
            Ok(Program { decls, body })
        } else {
            Err(p.errors)
        }
    }

    fn recover_decl(&mut self) {
        let mut depth: i32 = 0;
        while !matches!(self.cur().kind, Kind::Eof) {
            match self.cur().kind {
                Kind::P(P::LBrace) | Kind::P(P::LParen) | Kind::P(P::LBrack) => depth += 1,
                Kind::P(P::RBrace) | Kind::P(P::RParen) | Kind::P(P::RBrack) => {
                    depth -= 1;
                    if depth < 0 {
                        self.bump();
                        return;
                    }
                }
                Kind::P(P::Semi) if depth == 0 => {
                    self.bump();
                    return;
                }
                Kind::Kw(Kw::Let) | Kind::Kw(Kw::Type) | Kind::Kw(Kw::Bring) if depth == 0 => {
                    return;
                }
                _ => {}
            }
            self.bump();
        }
    }

    fn recover_block(&mut self) {
        let mut depth: i32 = 0;
        while !matches!(self.cur().kind, Kind::Eof) {
            match self.cur().kind {
                Kind::P(P::LBrace) | Kind::P(P::LParen) | Kind::P(P::LBrack) => depth += 1,
                Kind::P(P::RBrace) if depth == 0 => return,
                Kind::P(P::RBrace) | Kind::P(P::RParen) | Kind::P(P::RBrack) => {
                    depth -= 1;
                    if depth < 0 {
                        return;
                    }
                }
                Kind::P(P::Semi) if depth == 0 => {
                    self.bump();
                    if matches!(self.cur().kind, Kind::P(P::RBrace)) {
                        return;
                    }
                    continue;
                }
                _ => {}
            }
            self.bump();
        }
    }

    #[inline]
    fn cur(&self) -> Tok {
        self.toks[self.pos]
    }

    #[inline]
    fn peek(&self, n: usize) -> Tok {
        self.toks
            .get(self.pos + n)
            .copied()
            .unwrap_or_else(|| *self.toks.last().unwrap())
    }

    #[inline]
    fn bump(&mut self) -> Tok {
        let t = self.toks[self.pos];
        if self.pos + 1 < self.toks.len() {
            self.pos += 1;
        }
        t
    }

    #[inline]
    fn eat_p(&mut self, p: P) -> Option<Span> {
        if let Kind::P(q) = self.cur().kind {
            if q == p {
                return Some(self.bump().span);
            }
        }
        None
    }

    #[inline]
    fn eat_kw(&mut self, k: Kw) -> Option<Span> {
        if let Kind::Kw(q) = self.cur().kind {
            if q == k {
                return Some(self.bump().span);
            }
        }
        None
    }

    #[cold]
    fn err_expect(&self, what: &str) -> ParseErr {
        ParseErr {
            msg: format!("expected {}, found {}", what, describe(&self.cur())),
            span: self.cur().span,
            note: None,
        }
    }

    #[cold]
    fn err_expect_note(&self, what: &str, note: &str) -> ParseErr {
        ParseErr {
            msg: format!("expected {}, found {}", what, describe(&self.cur())),
            span: self.cur().span,
            note: Some(note.into()),
        }
    }

    #[inline]
    fn expect_p(&mut self, p: P, what: &str) -> PResult<Span> {
        self.eat_p(p).ok_or_else(|| self.err_expect(what))
    }

    #[inline]
    fn expect_kw(&mut self, k: Kw, what: &str) -> PResult<Span> {
        self.eat_kw(k).ok_or_else(|| self.err_expect(what))
    }

    #[inline]
    fn eat_expr_arrow(&mut self) -> Option<Span> {
        if let Some(span) = self.eat_p(P::Arrow) {
            return Some(span);
        }
        self.eat_p(P::FatArrow)
    }

    #[inline]
    fn expect_expr_arrow(&mut self, what: &str) -> PResult<Span> {
        self.eat_expr_arrow().ok_or_else(|| self.err_expect(what))
    }

    fn ident(&mut self) -> PResult<String> {
        if let Kind::Ident = self.cur().kind {
            let sp = self.bump().span;
            Ok(self.slice(sp).to_owned())
        } else {
            Err(self.err_expect("an identifier"))
        }
    }

    #[inline]
    fn slice(&self, s: Span) -> &str {
        unsafe { std::str::from_utf8_unchecked(&self.src[s.lo as usize..s.hi as usize]) }
    }

    fn is_soft_kw(&self, pos: usize, name: &[u8]) -> bool {
        if let Some(t) = self.toks.get(pos) {
            if let Kind::Ident = t.kind {
                return &self.src[t.span.lo as usize..t.span.hi as usize] == name;
            }
        }
        false
    }

    fn is_decl_start(&self) -> bool {
        matches!(
            self.cur().kind,
            Kind::Kw(Kw::Let) | Kind::Kw(Kw::Type) | Kind::Kw(Kw::Bring)
        )
    }

    fn decl(&mut self) -> PResult<Decl> {
        match self.cur().kind {
            Kind::Kw(Kw::Type) => self.decl_type(),
            Kind::Kw(Kw::Let) => self.decl_let(),
            Kind::Kw(Kw::Bring) => self.decl_bring(),
            _ => Err(self.err_expect("a top-level `let`, `type`, or `bring` declaration")),
        }
    }

    fn decl_bring(&mut self) -> PResult<Decl> {
        // Consume `bring`. The span is not needed by Decl::Bring.
        self.bump();

        let mut paths = Vec::new();
        while matches!(self.cur().kind, Kind::Str | Kind::StrEsc) {
            let sp = self.bump().span;
            let raw = &self.src[sp.lo as usize..sp.hi as usize];
            paths.push(String::from_utf8_lossy(&decode_string(raw)).into_owned());
        }

        if paths.is_empty() {
            return Err(self.err_expect("at least one string literal after `bring`"));
        }

        self.expect_p(P::Semi, "`;` after `bring`")?;
        Ok(Decl::Bring(paths))
    }

    fn decl_let(&mut self) -> PResult<Decl> {
        let start = self.bump().span;

        if matches!(self.cur().kind, Kind::Kw(Kw::Extern))
            || self.is_soft_kw(self.pos, b"external")
        {
            self.bump();
            return Ok(Decl::External(self.decl_external_body(start)?));
        }

        let name = self.ident()?;
        let mut params = Vec::new();

        while !matches!(self.cur().kind, Kind::P(P::Eq)) {
            if matches!(self.cur().kind, Kind::Ident) {
                params.push(self.ident()?);
            } else if matches!(self.cur().kind, Kind::Eof) {
                return Err(ParseErr {
                    msg: "unexpected end of input inside `let`".into(),
                    span: self.cur().span,
                    note: Some(format!("`{name}` needs a value: `let {name} = ...;`")),
                });
            } else {
                return Err(self.err_expect_note(
                    "`=` after the binding name",
                    "a top-level `let` takes the form `let name = value;`",
                ));
            }
        }

        self.expect_p(P::Eq, "`=` in `let`")?;
        let mut value = self.expr()?;

        if !params.is_empty() {
            let sp = value.span;
            value = Expr {
                span: sp,
                kind: ExprKind::Fn {
                    params,
                    body: Box::new(value),
                },
            };
        }

        self.expect_p(P::Semi, "`;` after `let`")?;
        Ok(Decl::Let {
            name,
            value: Box::new(value),
            span: start,
        })
    }

    fn decl_type(&mut self) -> PResult<Decl> {
        let span = self.bump().span;
        let name = self.ident()?;
        let mut params = Vec::new();

        while let Kind::Ident = self.cur().kind {
            params.push(self.ident()?);
        }

        if self.eat_p(P::Semi).is_some() {
            return Ok(Decl::Type {
                name,
                params,
                ctors: vec![],
                span,
            });
        }

        self.expect_p(P::LBrace, "`{` to open type body")?;
        let mut ctors = Vec::new();

        while self.cur().kind != Kind::P(P::RBrace) {
            if matches!(self.cur().kind, Kind::Eof) {
                return Err(ParseErr {
                    msg: format!("unterminated body of type `{name}`"),
                    span: self.cur().span,
                    note: Some("add a closing `}`".into()),
                });
            }

            let cspan = self.cur().span;
            let cname = self.ident()?;
            let mut args = Vec::new();

            while !matches!(self.cur().kind, Kind::P(P::Semi) | Kind::P(P::RBrace)) {
                args.push(self.ty()?);
            }

            self.eat_p(P::Semi);
            ctors.push(Ctor {
                name: cname,
                args,
                span: cspan,
            });
        }

        self.expect_p(P::RBrace, "`}` to close type body")?;
        self.expect_p(P::Semi, "`;` after type declaration")?;

        Ok(Decl::Type {
            name,
            params,
            ctors,
            span,
        })
    }

    fn decl_external_body(&mut self, start: Span) -> PResult<Extern> {
        let name = self.ident()?;

        let mak_ty = if self.eat_p(P::Colon).is_some() {
            self.ty()?
        } else {
            Ty::Bottom
        };

        self.expect_p(P::Eq, "`=` in external declaration")?;
        let is_global = self.is_soft_kw(self.pos, b"global");

        if is_global {
            self.bump();
        }

        let sym_span = match self.cur().kind {
            Kind::Str | Kind::StrEsc => self.bump().span,
            _ => {
                return Err(self.err_expect_note(
                    "a string literal symbol name",
                    "external declarations use `= \"symbol\"` to name the linked symbol",
                ));
            }
        };

        let symbol = String::from_utf8_lossy(
            &self.src[sym_span.lo as usize..sym_span.hi as usize],
        )
        .into_owned();

        let foreign = if self.eat_p(P::Colon).is_some() {
            self.foreign_ty()?
        } else {
            ForeignTy::Atom(ForeignAtom::Void)
        };

        let mut pure = false;
        let mut from = None;
        let mut abi = None;

        if self.eat_p(P::LBrace).is_some() {
            while self.cur().kind != Kind::P(P::RBrace) {
                if matches!(self.cur().kind, Kind::Eof) {
                    return Err(ParseErr {
                        msg: "unterminated descriptor block".into(),
                        span: self.cur().span,
                        note: Some("add a closing `}`".into()),
                    });
                }

                let key = self.ident()?;
                self.expect_p(P::Eq, "`=` in descriptor entry")?;

                match key.as_str() {
                    "pure" => {
                        let v = self.ident()?;
                        pure = v == "true";
                    }
                    "from" | "abi" => {
                        let sp = match self.cur().kind {
                            Kind::Str | Kind::StrEsc => self.bump().span,
                            _ => {
                                return Err(self.err_expect(
                                    "a string literal in descriptor",
                                ));
                            }
                        };

                        let v = String::from_utf8_lossy(
                            &self.src[sp.lo as usize..sp.hi as usize],
                        )
                        .into_owned();

                        if key == "from" {
                            from = Some(v);
                        } else {
                            abi = Some(v);
                        }
                    }
                    _ => {
                        let _ = self.expr()?;
                    }
                }

                self.eat_p(P::Semi);
            }

            self.expect_p(P::RBrace, "`}` in descriptor")?;
        }

        self.expect_p(P::Semi, "`;` after external declaration")?;

        Ok(Extern {
            name,
            mak_ty,
            symbol,
            is_global,
            foreign,
            pure,
            from,
            abi,
            span: start,
        })
    }

    fn ty(&mut self) -> PResult<Ty> {
        let lhs = self.ty_atom()?;

        if self.eat_p(P::Arrow).is_some() {
            let rhs = self.ty()?;
            return Ok(Ty::Fun(Box::new(lhs), Box::new(rhs)));
        }

        Ok(lhs)
    }

    fn ty_atom(&mut self) -> PResult<Ty> {
        match self.cur().kind {
            Kind::Ident => {
                let s = self.ident()?;

                Ok(match s.as_str() {
                    "u8" => Ty::Prim(Prim::U8),
                    "u16" => Ty::Prim(Prim::U16),
                    "u32" => Ty::Prim(Prim::U32),
                    "u64" => Ty::Prim(Prim::U64),
                    "i8" => Ty::Prim(Prim::I8),
                    "i16" => Ty::Prim(Prim::I16),
                    "i32" => Ty::Prim(Prim::I32),
                    "i64" => Ty::Prim(Prim::I64),
                    "f32" => Ty::Prim(Prim::F32),
                    "f64" => Ty::Prim(Prim::F64),
                    _ => Ty::Named(s),
                })
            }
            Kind::P(P::LBrack) => {
                self.bump();
                let t = self.ty()?;
                self.expect_p(P::RBrack, "`]` to close array type")?;
                Ok(Ty::Array(Box::new(t)))
            }
            Kind::P(P::LParen) => {
                self.bump();

                if self.eat_p(P::RParen).is_some() {
                    return Ok(Ty::Prim(Prim::Unit));
                }

                let first = self.ty()?;

                if self.eat_p(P::Comma).is_some() {
                    let mut ts = vec![first];
                    ts.push(self.ty()?);

                    while self.eat_p(P::Comma).is_some() {
                        ts.push(self.ty()?);
                    }

                    self.expect_p(P::RParen, "`)` to close tuple type")?;
                    return Ok(Ty::Tuple(ts));
                }

                if starts_ty_atom(&self.cur()) {
                    let name = match first {
                        Ty::Named(n) => n,
                        _ => {
                            return Err(ParseErr {
                                msg: "the head of a parenthesized type application must be a type name"
                                    .into(),
                                span: self.cur().span,
                                note: Some("write `(List a)` rather than `([u8] a)`".into()),
                            });
                        }
                    };

                    let mut args = vec![self.ty_atom()?];

                    while starts_ty_atom(&self.cur()) {
                        args.push(self.ty_atom()?);
                    }

                    self.expect_p(P::RParen, "`)` to close type application")?;
                    return Ok(Ty::App(name, args));
                }

                self.expect_p(P::RParen, "`)` to close parenthesized type")?;
                Ok(first)
            }
            Kind::P(P::Bang) => {
                self.bump();
                Ok(Ty::Bottom)
            }
            _ => Err(self.err_expect_note(
                "a type",
                "types are primitives (`u8`, `i64`, ...), names, `[T]`, `(T U)`, or `T -> U`",
            )),
        }
    }

    fn foreign_ty(&mut self) -> PResult<ForeignTy> {
        if self.eat_p(P::LParen).is_some() {
            let mut args = Vec::new();

            while self.cur().kind != Kind::P(P::RParen) {
                if matches!(self.cur().kind, Kind::Eof) {
                    return Err(ParseErr {
                        msg: "unterminated foreign function type".into(),
                        span: self.cur().span,
                        note: Some("add a closing `)` and a `->` return type".into()),
                    });
                }

                if self.cur().kind == Kind::P(P::Dot)
                    && self.peek(1).kind == Kind::P(P::Dot)
                    && self.peek(2).kind == Kind::P(P::Dot)
                {
                    self.bump();
                    self.bump();
                    self.bump();
                    args.push(ForeignArg::VarArgs);
                } else {
                    args.push(ForeignArg::Ty(self.foreign_ty()?));
                }

                if self.eat_p(P::Comma).is_none() {
                    break;
                }
            }

            self.expect_p(P::RParen, "`)` to close foreign arg list")?;
            self.expect_p(P::Arrow, "`->` before foreign return type")?;
            let ret = self.foreign_ty()?;

            return Ok(ForeignTy::Fun {
                args,
                ret: Box::new(ret),
            });
        }

        let atom = match self.cur().kind {
            Kind::Ident => {
                let s = self.ident()?;

                match s.as_str() {
                    "i8" => ForeignAtom::I8,
                    "i16" => ForeignAtom::I16,
                    "i32" => ForeignAtom::I32,
                    "i64" => ForeignAtom::I64,
                    "u8" => ForeignAtom::U8,
                    "u16" => ForeignAtom::U16,
                    "u32" => ForeignAtom::U32,
                    "u64" => ForeignAtom::U64,
                    "f32" => ForeignAtom::F32,
                    "f64" => ForeignAtom::F64,
                    "ptr" => ForeignAtom::Ptr,
                    "void" => ForeignAtom::Void,
                    _ => ForeignAtom::Named(s),
                }
            }
            _ => return Err(self.err_expect("a foreign type atom")),
        };

        // Accept repeated pointer stars (`i32*`, `i32**`, `u8***`).
        // Preserve each pointer layer so codegen can determine the element
        // type at every level of chained indexing.
        let mut ty = ForeignTy::Atom(atom);

        while self.eat_p(P::Star).is_some() {
            ty = ForeignTy::Atom(ForeignAtom::PtrTo(Box::new(ty)));
        }

        Ok(ty)
    }

    pub fn expr(&mut self) -> PResult<Expr> {
        self.expr_bp(0)
    }

    /// Parse prefix operators before entering the binary/application layer.
    ///
    /// The operand is parsed with a binding power above `.` and every binary
    /// operator, while implicit function application remains available.
    fn prefix_expr(&mut self) -> PResult<Expr> {
        let t = self.cur();

        let op = match t.kind {
            Kind::P(P::Minus) => Some(UnOp::Neg),
            Kind::P(P::Bang) => Some(UnOp::Not),
            Kind::P(P::Tilde) => Some(UnOp::BitNot),
            _ => None,
        };

        if let Some(op) = op {
            self.bump();

            // Keep application inside the unary operand, but stop before
            // the dot/composition layer and all binary operators.
            let e = self.expr_bp(10)?;
            let span = Span {
                lo: t.span.lo,
                hi: e.span.hi.max(t.span.hi),
            };

            return Ok(Expr {
                kind: ExprKind::Un {
                    op,
                    e: Box::new(e),
                },
                span,
            });
        }

        self.atom()
    }

    fn expr_bp(&mut self, min_bp: u8) -> PResult<Expr> {
        let mut lhs = self.prefix_expr()?;

        loop {
            // Postfix indexing binds more tightly than implicit application
            // and every infix operator. Lower indexing to a reserved AST
            // intrinsic so a new public AST node is not required.
            if matches!(self.cur().kind, Kind::P(P::LBrack)) {
                lhs = self.index_suffix(lhs)?;
                continue;
            }

            // Preserve the existing empty-call syntax: f().
            if matches!(self.cur().kind, Kind::P(P::LParen))
                && matches!(self.peek(1).kind, Kind::P(P::RParen))
            {
                let open = self.bump().span;
                let close = self.bump().span;
                let span = Span {
                    lo: lhs.span.lo,
                    hi: close.hi.max(open.hi),
                };

                lhs = Expr {
                    span,
                    kind: ExprKind::App {
                        fun: Box::new(lhs),
                        args: Vec::new(),
                    },
                };

                continue;
            }

            if self.starts_atom() {
                // Parse indexing on the argument first, so `f xs[i]`
                // means `f (xs[i])`. Keep the intermediate in a local:
                // nesting self.atom() inside self.index_suffix() would
                // borrow self mutably twice in the same expression.
                let atom = self.atom()?;
                let arg = self.index_suffix(atom)?;

                let span = Span {
                    lo: lhs.span.lo,
                    hi: arg.span.hi,
                };

                lhs = Expr {
                    span,
                    kind: ExprKind::App {
                        fun: Box::new(lhs),
                        args: vec![arg],
                    },
                };

                continue;
            }

            let (op, lbp, rbp): (Option<BinOp>, u8, u8) = match self.cur().kind {
                Kind::P(P::Dot) => (None, 9, 9),
                Kind::P(P::Star) => (Some(BinOp::Mul), 7, 8),
                Kind::P(P::Slash) => (Some(BinOp::Div), 7, 8),
                Kind::P(P::Percent) => (Some(BinOp::Rem), 7, 8),
                Kind::P(P::Plus) => (Some(BinOp::Add), 6, 7),
                Kind::P(P::Minus) => (Some(BinOp::Sub), 6, 7),
                Kind::P(P::Shl) => (Some(BinOp::Shl), 5, 6),
                Kind::P(P::Shr) => (Some(BinOp::Shr), 5, 6),
                Kind::P(P::Amp) => (Some(BinOp::And), 4, 5),
                Kind::P(P::Caret) => (Some(BinOp::Xor), 4, 5),
                Kind::P(P::Pipe) => (Some(BinOp::Or), 4, 5),
                Kind::P(P::EqEq) => (Some(BinOp::Eq), 3, 4),
                Kind::P(P::Ne) => (Some(BinOp::Ne), 3, 4),
                Kind::P(P::Lt) => (Some(BinOp::Lt), 3, 4),
                Kind::P(P::Gt) => (Some(BinOp::Gt), 3, 4),
                Kind::P(P::Le) => (Some(BinOp::Le), 3, 4),
                Kind::P(P::Ge) => (Some(BinOp::Ge), 3, 4),
                Kind::P(P::AndAnd) => (Some(BinOp::LAnd), 2, 3),
                Kind::P(P::OrOr) => (Some(BinOp::LOr), 1, 2),
                _ => break,
            };

            if lbp < min_bp {
                break;
            }

            if op.is_none() {
                self.bump();
                let rhs = self.expr_bp(rbp)?;
                let span = Span {
                    lo: lhs.span.lo,
                    hi: rhs.span.hi,
                };
                let x = "$x".to_string();
                let xvar = || Expr {
                    span,
                    kind: ExprKind::Var(x.clone()),
                };

                lhs = Expr {
                    span,
                    kind: ExprKind::Fn {
                        params: vec![x.clone()],
                        body: Box::new(Expr {
                            span,
                            kind: ExprKind::App {
                                fun: Box::new(lhs),
                                args: vec![Expr {
                                    span,
                                    kind: ExprKind::App {
                                        fun: Box::new(rhs),
                                        args: vec![xvar()],
                                    },
                                }],
                            },
                        }),
                    },
                };

                continue;
            }

            self.bump();
            let rhs = self.expr_bp(rbp)?;
            let span = Span {
                lo: lhs.span.lo,
                hi: rhs.span.hi,
            };

            lhs = Expr {
                span,
                kind: ExprKind::Bin {
                    op: op.unwrap(),
                    l: Box::new(lhs),
                    r: Box::new(rhs),
                },
            };
        }

        Ok(lhs)
    }

    /// Parse postfix indexing suffixes from an already parsed expression.
    /// `base[i][j]` becomes `$mak.index($mak.index(base, i), j)`.
    fn index_suffix(&mut self, mut base: Expr) -> PResult<Expr> {
        while matches!(self.cur().kind, Kind::P(P::LBrack)) {
            let open = self.bump().span;

            if matches!(self.cur().kind, Kind::P(P::RBrack)) {
                return Err(ParseErr {
                    msg: "an index expression cannot be empty".into(),
                    span: self.cur().span,
                    note: Some(
                        "write `value[index]`; standalone `[]` array literals are not supported"
                            .into(),
                    ),
                });
            }

            let index = self.expr()?;
            let close = self.expect_p(P::RBrack, "`]` to close index expression")?;

            let span = Span {
                lo: base.span.lo.min(open.lo),
                hi: close.hi.max(index.span.hi),
            };

            base = Expr {
                span,
                kind: ExprKind::App {
                    fun: Box::new(Expr {
                        span: open,
                        kind: ExprKind::Var("$mak.index".to_string()),
                    }),
                    args: vec![base, index],
                },
            };
        }

        Ok(base)
    }

    fn starts_atom(&self) -> bool {
        matches!(
            self.cur().kind,
            Kind::Ident
                | Kind::Int(_)
                | Kind::Float(_)
                | Kind::Str
                | Kind::StrEsc
                | Kind::P(P::LParen)
                | Kind::P(P::LBrace)
                | Kind::P(P::Backslash)
                | Kind::Kw(Kw::Fn)
                | Kind::Kw(Kw::Let)
                | Kind::Kw(Kw::Case)
        )
    }

    fn atom(&mut self) -> PResult<Expr> {
        let t = self.cur();

        match t.kind {
            Kind::Int(v) => {
                self.bump();
                Ok(Expr {
                    kind: ExprKind::Int(v),
                    span: t.span,
                })
            }
            Kind::Float(v) => {
                self.bump();
                Ok(Expr {
                    kind: ExprKind::Float(v),
                    span: t.span,
                })
            }
            Kind::Str | Kind::StrEsc => {
                self.bump();
                let raw = &self.src[t.span.lo as usize..t.span.hi as usize];

                Ok(Expr {
                    kind: ExprKind::Str(decode_string(raw)),
                    span: t.span,
                })
            }
            Kind::Ident => {
                let name = self.ident()?;
                Ok(Expr {
                    kind: ExprKind::Var(name),
                    span: t.span,
                })
            }
            Kind::P(P::LParen) => {
                self.bump();
                let e = self.expr()?;
                self.expect_p(P::RParen, "`)` to close parenthesized expression")?;
                Ok(e)
            }
            Kind::P(P::LBrack) => Err(ParseErr {
                msg: "unexpected `[`; indexing must follow an expression".into(),
                span: t.span,
                note: Some(
                    "write `value[index]`; standalone `[]` array literals are not supported"
                        .into(),
                ),
            }),
            Kind::P(P::LBrace) => {
                self.bump();
                let kind = self.block_body()?;
                self.expect_p(P::RBrace, "`}` to close block")?;
                Ok(Expr { span: t.span, kind })
            }
            Kind::Kw(Kw::Let) => self.let_expr(),
            Kind::Kw(Kw::Fn) | Kind::P(P::Backslash) => self.fn_expr(),
            Kind::Kw(Kw::Case) => self.case_expr(),
            _ => Err(self.err_expect_note(
                "an expression",
                "expressions are literals, identifiers, `(expr)`, `{...}`, `fn`, `case`, or `let`",
            )),
        }
    }

    fn block_body(&mut self) -> PResult<ExprKind> {
        let mut stmts: Vec<Expr> = Vec::new();

        loop {
            if matches!(self.cur().kind, Kind::P(P::RBrace)) {
                if stmts.is_empty() {
                    return Ok(ExprKind::Int(0));
                }

                let tail = stmts.pop().unwrap();

                if stmts.is_empty() {
                    return Ok(tail.kind);
                }

                return Ok(ExprKind::Block {
                    stmts,
                    tail: Box::new(tail),
                });
            }

            if matches!(self.cur().kind, Kind::Eof) {
                return Err(ParseErr {
                    msg: "unterminated block: missing `}`".into(),
                    span: self.cur().span,
                    note: Some("every `{` needs a matching `}`".into()),
                });
            }

            if matches!(self.cur().kind, Kind::Kw(Kw::Let)) {
                let sp = self.cur().span;
                let let_kind = self.block_let()?;
                let let_expr = Expr {
                    span: sp,
                    kind: let_kind,
                };

                if stmts.is_empty() {
                    return Ok(let_expr.kind);
                }

                return Ok(ExprKind::Block {
                    stmts,
                    tail: Box::new(let_expr),
                });
            }

            let e = match self.expr() {
                Ok(e) => e,
                Err(err) => {
                    self.errors.push(err);
                    self.recover_block();

                    if matches!(self.cur().kind, Kind::P(P::RBrace)) {
                        if stmts.is_empty() {
                            return Ok(ExprKind::Int(0));
                        }

                        let tail = stmts.pop().unwrap();

                        if stmts.is_empty() {
                            return Ok(tail.kind);
                        }

                        return Ok(ExprKind::Block {
                            stmts,
                            tail: Box::new(tail),
                        });
                    }

                    continue;
                }
            };

            if self.eat_p(P::Semi).is_none() {
                if stmts.is_empty() {
                    return Ok(e.kind);
                }

                return Ok(ExprKind::Block {
                    stmts,
                    tail: Box::new(e),
                });
            }

            stmts.push(e);
        }
    }

    fn block_let(&mut self) -> PResult<ExprKind> {
        let sp = self.bump().span;

        if matches!(self.cur().kind, Kind::Kw(Kw::Extern))
            || self.is_soft_kw(self.pos, b"external")
        {
            return Err(ParseErr {
                msg: "`external` declarations may only appear at the top level".into(),
                span: self.cur().span,
                note: Some("move this declaration outside of any function body".into()),
            });
        }

        let name = self.ident()?;
        let mut params = Vec::new();

        while !matches!(self.cur().kind, Kind::P(P::Eq)) {
            match self.cur().kind {
                Kind::Ident => params.push(self.ident()?),
                _ => {
                    return Err(self.err_expect_note(
                        "`=` in `let` binding",
                        "a local binding takes the form `let name = value;`",
                    ));
                }
            }
        }

        self.expect_p(P::Eq, "`=` in `let` binding")?;
        let mut value = self.expr()?;

        if !params.is_empty() {
            let vs = value.span;
            value = Expr {
                span: vs,
                kind: ExprKind::Fn {
                    params,
                    body: Box::new(value),
                },
            };
        }

        self.expect_p(P::Semi, "`;` after `let` binding")?;
        let body_kind = self.block_body()?;
        let body = Expr {
            span: sp,
            kind: body_kind,
        };

        Ok(ExprKind::Let {
            name,
            value: Box::new(value),
            body: Box::new(body),
        })
    }

    fn let_expr(&mut self) -> PResult<Expr> {
        let sp = self.bump().span;
        let name = self.ident()?;
        let mut params = Vec::new();

        while !matches!(self.cur().kind, Kind::P(P::Eq)) {
            match self.cur().kind {
                Kind::Ident => params.push(self.ident()?),
                _ => {
                    return Err(self.err_expect_note(
                        "`=` in `let` binding",
                        "a `let` expression takes the form `let name = value; body`",
                    ));
                }
            }
        }

        self.expect_p(P::Eq, "`=` in `let` binding")?;
        let mut value = self.expr()?;

        if !params.is_empty() {
            let vs = value.span;
            value = Expr {
                span: vs,
                kind: ExprKind::Fn {
                    params,
                    body: Box::new(value),
                },
            };
        }

        self.expect_p(P::Semi, "`;` after `let` binding")?;
        let body = self.expr()?;
        let span = Span {
            lo: sp.lo,
            hi: body.span.hi,
        };

        Ok(Expr {
            span,
            kind: ExprKind::Let {
                name,
                value: Box::new(value),
                body: Box::new(body),
            },
        })
    }

    fn fn_expr(&mut self) -> PResult<Expr> {
        let sp = self.bump().span;
        let mut params = Vec::new();

        if self.eat_p(P::LParen).is_some() {
            while !matches!(self.cur().kind, Kind::P(P::RParen)) {
                if matches!(self.cur().kind, Kind::Eof) {
                    return Err(ParseErr {
                        msg: "unterminated parameter list: missing `)`".into(),
                        span: self.cur().span,
                        note: Some("parameter lists look like `fn (a b c) { ... }`".into()),
                    });
                }

                match self.cur().kind {
                    Kind::Ident => params.push(self.ident()?),
                    _ => {
                        return Err(self.err_expect_note(
                            "a parameter name",
                            "parameters are identifiers, optionally separated by commas",
                        ));
                    }
                }

                self.eat_p(P::Comma);
            }

            self.expect_p(P::RParen, "`)` to close parameter list")?;
        } else {
            while let Kind::Ident = self.cur().kind {
                params.push(self.ident()?);
            }
        }

        let body = if self.eat_expr_arrow().is_some() {
            self.expr()?
        } else {
            let open = match self.eat_p(P::LBrace) {
                Some(s) => s,
                None => {
                    return Err(self.err_expect_note(
                        "`{`, `->`, or `=>` after parameters",
                        "write `fn (a b) { body }`, `fn (a b) -> body`, or `fn (a b) => body`",
                    ));
                }
            };

            let kind = self.block_body()?;
            self.expect_p(P::RBrace, "`}` to close function body")?;
            Expr { span: open, kind }
        };

        let span = Span {
            lo: sp.lo,
            hi: body.span.hi,
        };

        Ok(Expr {
            span,
            kind: ExprKind::Fn {
                params,
                body: Box::new(body),
            },
        })
    }

    fn case_expr(&mut self) -> PResult<Expr> {
        let sp = self.bump().span;
        let scrut = self.expr()?;

        self.expect_kw(Kw::Of, "`of` after `case` scrutinee")?;
        self.expect_p(P::LBrace, "`{` to open case arms")?;

        let mut arms = Vec::new();

        while self.cur().kind != Kind::P(P::RBrace) {
            if matches!(self.cur().kind, Kind::Eof) {
                return Err(ParseErr {
                    msg: "unterminated `case`: missing `}`".into(),
                    span: self.cur().span,
                    note: Some("each arm is `pattern -> body;`".into()),
                });
            }

            let asp = self.cur().span;
            let pat = self.pat()?;
            self.expect_expr_arrow("`->` or `=>` in case arm")?;
            let body = self.expr()?;
            self.eat_p(P::Semi);

            arms.push(Arm {
                pat,
                body,
                span: asp,
            });
        }

        self.expect_p(P::RBrace, "`}` to close case")?;

        let span = Span {
            lo: sp.lo,
            hi: self.cur().span.hi,
        };

        Ok(Expr {
            span,
            kind: ExprKind::Case {
                scrut: Box::new(scrut),
                arms,
            },
        })
    }

    fn pat(&mut self) -> PResult<Pat> {
        match self.cur().kind {
            Kind::Ident => {
                let name = self.ident()?;

                if name == "_" {
                    return Ok(Pat::Wild);
                }

                if name.as_bytes()[0].is_ascii_uppercase() {
                    let mut args = Vec::new();

                    while self.starts_atom() {
                        args.push(self.pat()?);
                    }

                    Ok(Pat::Ctor { name, args })
                } else {
                    Ok(Pat::Bind(name))
                }
            }
            Kind::Int(v) => {
                self.bump();
                Ok(Pat::Int(v))
            }
            Kind::Str | Kind::StrEsc => {
                let sp = self.bump().span;
                let raw = &self.src[sp.lo as usize..sp.hi as usize];
                Ok(Pat::Str(decode_string(raw)))
            }
            Kind::P(P::LParen) => {
                self.bump();

                if self.eat_p(P::RParen).is_some() {
                    return Ok(Pat::Tuple(vec![]));
                }

                let mut ps = vec![self.pat()?];

                while self.eat_p(P::Comma).is_some() {
                    ps.push(self.pat()?);
                }

                self.expect_p(P::RParen, "`)` to close tuple pattern")?;

                Ok(if ps.len() == 1 {
                    ps.pop().unwrap()
                } else {
                    Pat::Tuple(ps)
                })
            }
            _ => Err(self.err_expect_note(
                "a pattern",
                "patterns are `_`, a binding, a literal, `Ctor p1 p2`, or `(p1, p2)`",
            )),
        }
    }
}

fn starts_ty_atom(t: &Tok) -> bool {
    matches!(
        t.kind,
        Kind::Ident | Kind::P(P::LParen) | Kind::P(P::LBrack)
    )
}

fn describe(t: &Tok) -> String {
    match &t.kind {
        Kind::Eof => "end of input".into(),
        Kind::Ident => "an identifier".into(),
        Kind::Int(_) => "an integer".into(),
        Kind::Float(_) => "a float".into(),
        Kind::Str | Kind::StrEsc => "a string".into(),
        Kind::Kw(k) => format!("keyword `{}`", kw_str(*k)),
        Kind::P(p) => format!("`{}`", p_str(*p)),
        Kind::Err(_) => "a lexer error".into(),
    }
}

fn kw_str(k: Kw) -> &'static str {
    match k {
        Kw::Let => "let",
        Kw::Fn => "fn",
        Kw::Case => "case",
        Kw::Type => "type",
        Kw::Extern => "extern",
        Kw::Of => "of",
        Kw::Pass => "pass",
        Kw::Bring => "bring",
    }
}

fn p_str(p: P) -> &'static str {
    match p {
        P::LBrace => "{",
        P::RBrace => "}",
        P::LParen => "(",
        P::RParen => ")",
        P::LBrack => "[",
        P::RBrack => "]",
        P::Semi => ";",
        P::Comma => ",",
        P::Colon => ":",
        P::Dot => ".",
        P::Backslash => "\\",
        P::Arrow => "->",
        P::FatArrow => "=>",
        P::Eq => "=",
        P::Plus => "+",
        P::Minus => "-",
        P::Star => "*",
        P::Slash => "/",
        P::Percent => "%",
        P::Amp => "&",
        P::Pipe => "|",
        P::Caret => "^",
        P::Tilde => "~",
        P::Shl => "<<",
        P::Shr => ">>",
        P::EqEq => "==",
        P::Ne => "/=",
        P::Lt => "<",
        P::Gt => ">",
        P::Le => "<=",
        P::Ge => ">=",
        P::AndAnd => "&&",
        P::OrOr => "||",
        P::Bang => "!",
    }
}

fn printable(b: u8) -> char {
    if b.is_ascii_graphic() {
        b as char
    } else {
        '?'
    }
}

fn decode_string(raw: &[u8]) -> Vec<u8> {
    let mut out = Vec::with_capacity(raw.len());
    let mut i = 0;

    while i < raw.len() {
        let b = raw[i];

        if b == b'\\' && i + 1 < raw.len() {
            i += 1;

            match raw[i] {
                b'n' => out.push(b'\n'),
                b't' => out.push(b'\t'),
                b'r' => out.push(b'\r'),
                b'\\' => out.push(b'\\'),
                b'"' => out.push(b'"'),
                b'\'' => out.push(b'\''),
                b'0' => out.push(0),
                b'x' if i + 2 < raw.len() => {
                    let h = hex(raw[i + 1]);
                    let l = hex(raw[i + 2]);

                    if let (Some(h), Some(l)) = (h, l) {
                        out.push((h << 4) | l);
                        i += 2;
                    } else {
                        out.push(b'x');
                    }
                }
                x => out.push(x),
            }
        } else {
            out.push(b);
        }

        i += 1;
    }

    out
}

#[inline]
fn hex(b: u8) -> Option<u8> {
    match b {
        b'0'..=b'9' => Some(b - b'0'),
        b'a'..=b'f' => Some(b - b'a' + 10),
        b'A'..=b'F' => Some(b - b'A' + 10),
        _ => None,
    }
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod indexing_regressions {
    use super::*;

    fn is_index(e: &Expr) -> bool {
        matches!(
            &e.kind,
            ExprKind::App { fun, args }
                if matches!(&fun.kind, ExprKind::Var(name) if name == "$mak.index")
                    && args.len() == 2
        )
    }

    #[test]
    fn parses_simple_and_chained_postfix_indexing() {
        let program = Parser::parse(b"matrix[row][column]").expect("valid index expression");

        let ExprKind::App { args, .. } = &program.body.kind else {
            panic!("outer index must be an intrinsic application");
        };

        assert_eq!(args.len(), 2);
        assert!(is_index(&args[0]), "the left side should be the inner index");
        assert!(matches!(&args[1].kind, ExprKind::Var(n) if n == "column"));
    }

    #[test]
    fn indexing_binds_tighter_than_implicit_application() {
        let program = Parser::parse(b"f values[index]").expect("valid application");

        let ExprKind::App { fun, args } = &program.body.kind else {
            panic!("expression should be a function application");
        };

        assert!(matches!(&fun.kind, ExprKind::Var(n) if n == "f"));
        assert_eq!(args.len(), 1);
        assert!(is_index(&args[0]));
    }

    #[test]
    fn rejects_empty_index_syntax_instead_of_treating_it_as_zero() {
        assert!(Parser::parse(b"values[]").is_err());
        assert!(Parser::parse(b"[]").is_err());
    }

    #[test]
    fn preserves_repeated_pointer_stars_in_external_types() {
        let program = Parser::parse(
            b"let extern matrix : [i32] = \"matrix\" : i32**; matrix[0][1]",
        )
        .expect("valid typed external pointer declaration");

        let Decl::External(external) = &program.decls[0] else {
            panic!("expected external declaration");
        };

        let ForeignTy::Atom(ForeignAtom::PtrTo(inner)) = &external.foreign else {
            panic!("outer pointer layer was lost");
        };

        assert!(matches!(
            inner.as_ref(),
            ForeignTy::Atom(ForeignAtom::PtrTo(_))
        ));
        assert!(is_index(&program.body));
    }
}

use crate::ast::*;
use crate::lexer::Span;
use std::collections::{HashMap, HashSet};

/// Internal names emitted by the parser for syntax that does not have
/// its own AST node. These are compiler intrinsics, not user variables.
const BUILTIN_INTRINSICS: &[&str] = &["$mak.index"];

#[inline]
fn is_builtin_intrinsic(name: &str) -> bool {
    BUILTIN_INTRINSICS.contains(&name)
}

/// Enable semantic-analysis tracing by setting MAK_DEBUG_SEMA=1.
///
/// Tracing is disabled by default. Values such as 0, false, off, and no
/// leave it disabled.
fn sema_debug_enabled() -> bool {
    std::env::var("MAK_DEBUG_SEMA")
        .map(|value| {
            let value = value.trim();
            !value.is_empty()
                && !matches!(
                    value.to_ascii_lowercase().as_str(),
                    "0" | "false" | "off" | "no"
                )
        })
        .unwrap_or(false)
}

#[inline]
fn sema_debug(enabled: bool, message: impl std::fmt::Display) {
    if enabled {
        #[cfg(debug_assertions)]
        eprintln!("[sema] {message}");
    }
}

fn decl_label(decl: &Decl) -> String {
    match decl {
        Decl::Let { name, .. } => format!("let {name}"),
        Decl::External(Extern { name, .. }) => format!("external {name}"),
        Decl::Type { name, .. } => format!("type {name}"),
        Decl::Bring(paths) => format!("bring {} path(s)", paths.len()),
    }
}

#[derive(Debug, Clone)]
pub struct SemaErr {
    pub msg: String,
    pub span: Span,
    pub note: Option<String>,
}

fn levenshtein(a: &str, b: &str) -> usize {
    let mut d = vec![vec![0; b.len() + 1]; a.len() + 1];

    for i in 0..=a.len() {
        d[i][0] = i;
    }

    for j in 0..=b.len() {
        d[0][j] = j;
    }

    for i in 1..=a.len() {
        for j in 1..=b.len() {
            let cost = if a.as_bytes()[i - 1] == b.as_bytes()[j - 1] {
                0
            } else {
                1
            };

            d[i][j] = (d[i - 1][j] + 1)
                .min(d[i][j - 1] + 1)
                .min(d[i - 1][j - 1] + cost);
        }
    }

    d[a.len()][b.len()]
}

pub fn analyze(mut p: Program) -> Result<Program, Vec<SemaErr>> {
    let debug = sema_debug_enabled();
    let mut errors = Vec::new();

    sema_debug(
        debug,
        format!(
            "starting analysis: {} top-level declaration(s), body span {}..{}",
            p.decls.len(),
            p.body.span.lo,
            p.body.span.hi
        ),
    );

    if debug {
        for (index, decl) in p.decls.iter().enumerate() {
            eprintln!(
                "[sema] input declaration #{index}: {}",
                decl_label(decl)
            );
        }
    }

    // =====================================================================
    // PHASE 1: Reachability
    //
    // Remove unused top-level declarations while preserving the declarations
    // referenced by the main expression and their dependencies.
    //
    // IMPORTANT:
    // Parser-generated intrinsics such as $mak.index do not name declarations.
    // They must not become roots in the declaration-reachability analysis.
    // =====================================================================

    let mut used = HashSet::new();
    let mut top_level: HashMap<String, &Decl> = HashMap::new();

    for decl in &p.decls {
        match decl {
            Decl::Let { name, .. } | Decl::External(Extern { name, .. }) => {
                top_level.insert(name.clone(), decl);
            }

            Decl::Type { name, ctors, .. } => {
                top_level.insert(name.clone(), decl);

                for ctor in ctors {
                    top_level.insert(ctor.name.clone(), decl);
                }
            }

            Decl::Bring(_) => {}
        }
    }

    fn walk_expr_used(expr: &Expr, used: &mut HashSet<String>) {
        match &expr.kind {
            ExprKind::Var(name) => {
                // Intrinsics are handled by the compiler and do not correspond
                // to source-level declarations that can be retained or removed.
                if !is_builtin_intrinsic(name) {
                    used.insert(name.clone());
                }
            }

            ExprKind::Let { value, body, .. } => {
                walk_expr_used(value, used);
                walk_expr_used(body, used);
            }

            ExprKind::Fn { body, .. } => {
                walk_expr_used(body, used);
            }

            ExprKind::App { fun, args } => {
                walk_expr_used(fun, used);

                for arg in args {
                    walk_expr_used(arg, used);
                }
            }

            ExprKind::Case { scrut, arms } => {
                walk_expr_used(scrut, used);

                for arm in arms {
                    walk_expr_used(&arm.body, used);
                }
            }

            ExprKind::Block { stmts, tail } => {
                for stmt in stmts {
                    walk_expr_used(stmt, used);
                }

                walk_expr_used(tail, used);
            }

            ExprKind::Bin { l, r, .. } => {
                walk_expr_used(l, used);
                walk_expr_used(r, used);
            }

            ExprKind::Un { e, .. } | ExprKind::Quote(e) => {
                walk_expr_used(e, used);
            }

            ExprKind::Int(_) | ExprKind::Float(_) | ExprKind::Str(_) => {}
        }
    }

    walk_expr_used(&p.body, &mut used);

    if debug {
        let mut names: Vec<String> = used.iter().cloned().collect();
        names.sort();

        sema_debug(debug, format!("initial reachable names: {names:?}"));
    }

    // Follow dependencies of reachable top-level let bindings until no new
    // names are discovered.
    let mut changed = true;

    while changed {
        changed = false;

        let currently_used: Vec<String> = used.iter().cloned().collect();

        for name in currently_used {
            if let Some(decl) = top_level.get(&name) {
                if let Decl::Let { value, .. } = decl {
                    let before = used.len();

                    walk_expr_used(value, &mut used);

                    if used.len() > before {
                        changed = true;
                    }
                }
            }
        }
    }

    let before_reachability = p.decls.len();

    p.decls.retain(|decl| match decl {
        Decl::Let { name, .. } | Decl::External(Extern { name, .. }) => {
            used.contains(name)
        }

        Decl::Type { name, ctors, .. } => {
            used.contains(name)
                || ctors.iter().any(|ctor| used.contains(&ctor.name))
        }

        // Keep bring declarations, because they may affect how the source
        // program is loaded or interpreted.
        Decl::Bring(_) => true,
    });

    if debug {
        sema_debug(
            debug,
            format!(
                "reachability retained {} of {} declaration(s)",
                p.decls.len(),
                before_reachability
            ),
        );

        for decl in &p.decls {
            eprintln!("[sema] retained: {}", decl_label(decl));
        }

        let mut names: Vec<String> = used.iter().cloned().collect();
        names.sort();

        sema_debug(debug, format!("final reachable names: {names:?}"));
    }

    // =====================================================================
    // PHASE 2: Build the set of globally available source-level names.
    // =====================================================================

    let mut globals = HashSet::new();

    for decl in &p.decls {
        match decl {
            Decl::Let { name, .. } | Decl::External(Extern { name, .. }) => {
                globals.insert(name.clone());
            }

            Decl::Type { ctors, .. } => {
                for ctor in ctors {
                    globals.insert(ctor.name.clone());
                }
            }

            Decl::Bring(_) => {}
        }
    }

    if debug {
        let mut names: Vec<String> = globals.iter().cloned().collect();
        names.sort();

        sema_debug(debug, format!("global names: {names:?}"));
    }

    // =====================================================================
    // PHASE 3: Scope checking
    //
    // Report truly undefined variables. Recognize compiler intrinsics as
    // built-ins, without making arbitrary unknown names valid.
    // =====================================================================

    fn check_expr(
        expr: &Expr,
        scope: &mut Vec<HashSet<String>>,
        globals: &HashSet<String>,
        errors: &mut Vec<SemaErr>,
        debug: bool,
    ) {
        match &expr.kind {
            ExprKind::Var(name) => {
                let is_local = scope.iter().rev().any(|s| s.contains(name));
                let is_global = globals.contains(name);
                let is_builtin = is_builtin_intrinsic(name);

                let in_scope = is_local || is_global || is_builtin;

                if debug {
                    let resolution = if is_local {
                        "local"
                    } else if is_global {
                        "global"
                    } else if is_builtin {
                        "built-in intrinsic"
                    } else {
                        "UNDEFINED"
                    };

                    eprintln!(
                        "[sema] resolve `{name}` at {}..{} => {resolution}",
                        expr.span.lo,
                        expr.span.hi
                    );
                }

                if !in_scope {
                    let mut suggestions: Vec<String> = globals
                        .iter()
                        .filter(|candidate| {
                            let distance = levenshtein(name, candidate);
                            distance > 0 && distance <= 2
                        })
                        .cloned()
                        .collect();

                    suggestions.sort_by_key(|candidate| levenshtein(name, candidate));

                    let note = if suggestions.is_empty() {
                        Some(
                            "if this is a function, ensure it is defined or imported via `bring`"
                                .into(),
                        )
                    } else {
                        Some(format!("did you mean `{}`?", suggestions[0]))
                    };

                    errors.push(SemaErr {
                        msg: format!("undefined variable `{name}`"),
                        span: expr.span,
                        note,
                    });
                }
            }

            ExprKind::Let { name, value, body } => {
                // A local let value cannot see the name being introduced.
                check_expr(value, scope, globals, errors, debug);

                scope.last_mut().unwrap().insert(name.clone());

                check_expr(body, scope, globals, errors, debug);

                scope.last_mut().unwrap().remove(name);
            }

            ExprKind::Fn { params, body } => {
                scope.push(HashSet::new());

                for param in params {
                    scope.last_mut().unwrap().insert(param.clone());
                }

                check_expr(body, scope, globals, errors, debug);

                scope.pop();
            }

            ExprKind::App { fun, args } => {
                check_expr(fun, scope, globals, errors, debug);

                for arg in args {
                    check_expr(arg, scope, globals, errors, debug);
                }
            }

            ExprKind::Case { scrut, arms } => {
                check_expr(scrut, scope, globals, errors, debug);

                for arm in arms {
                    scope.push(HashSet::new());

                    collect_pat_vars(&arm.pat, scope.last_mut().unwrap());

                    check_expr(&arm.body, scope, globals, errors, debug);

                    scope.pop();
                }
            }

            ExprKind::Block { stmts, tail } => {
                scope.push(HashSet::new());

                for stmt in stmts {
                    check_expr(stmt, scope, globals, errors, debug);

                    // Block-local let declarations become visible to following
                    // statements, not to their own initializers.
                    if let ExprKind::Let { name, .. } = &stmt.kind {
                        scope.last_mut().unwrap().insert(name.clone());
                    }
                }

                check_expr(tail, scope, globals, errors, debug);

                scope.pop();
            }

            ExprKind::Bin { l, r, .. } => {
                check_expr(l, scope, globals, errors, debug);
                check_expr(r, scope, globals, errors, debug);
            }

            ExprKind::Un { e, .. } | ExprKind::Quote(e) => {
                check_expr(e, scope, globals, errors, debug);
            }

            ExprKind::Int(_) | ExprKind::Float(_) | ExprKind::Str(_) => {}
        }
    }

    fn collect_pat_vars(pattern: &Pat, out: &mut HashSet<String>) {
        match pattern {
            Pat::Bind(name) => {
                out.insert(name.clone());
            }

            Pat::Ctor { args, .. } | Pat::Tuple(args) => {
                for arg in args {
                    collect_pat_vars(arg, out);
                }
            }

            Pat::Wild | Pat::Int(_) | Pat::Str(_) => {}
        }
    }

    // Check top-level let expressions. External declarations contain type
    // metadata rather than a MAK expression body, so they need no scope walk.
    for decl in &p.decls {
        if let Decl::Let { value, .. } = decl {
            let mut scope = vec![HashSet::new()];

            if debug {
                sema_debug(
                    debug,
                    format!(
                        "checking top-level let value at {}..{}",
                        value.span.lo, value.span.hi
                    ),
                );
            }

            check_expr(value, &mut scope, &globals, &mut errors, debug);
        }
    }

    let mut scope = vec![HashSet::new()];

    sema_debug(
        debug,
        format!(
            "checking program body at {}..{}",
            p.body.span.lo, p.body.span.hi
        ),
    );

    check_expr(&p.body, &mut scope, &globals, &mut errors, debug);

    if debug {
        sema_debug(
            debug,
            format!("scope check found {} error(s)", errors.len()),
        );

        for error in &errors {
            eprintln!(
                "[sema] error at {}..{}: {}{}",
                error.span.lo,
                error.span.hi,
                error.msg,
                error
                    .note
                    .as_deref()
                    .map(|note| format!(" (note: {note})"))
                    .unwrap_or_default()
            );
        }
    }

    if errors.is_empty() {
        sema_debug(debug, "semantic analysis succeeded");
        Ok(p)
    } else {
        sema_debug(debug, "semantic analysis failed");
        Err(errors)
    }
}

┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄ :— 
use crate::ast::*;
use std::cell::RefCell;
use std::collections::{BTreeMap, BTreeSet, HashMap, HashSet};
use std::fmt::Write;
use std::fs;
use std::io;
use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};

#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Hash)]
enum Rep {
    Unknown,
    Unit,
    I8,
    I16,
    I32,
    I64,
    U8,
    U16,
    U32,
    U64,
    F32,
    F64,
    Str,
    Ptr,
    Adt(String),
    Function(usize),
    /// A first-class foreign function value.  Unlike `Rep::Ptr`, this keeps the
    /// exact callable C type and the fixed argument/return representations so
    /// aliases such as `let f = putchar; f 65` remain directly callable after
    /// optimization.
    ExternFunction {
        ctype: String,
        args: Vec<Rep>,
        variadic: bool,
        ret: Box<Rep>,
    },
}

impl Rep {
    #[inline]
    fn is_integer(&self) -> bool {
        matches!(
            self,
            Self::I8
                | Self::I16
                | Self::I32
                | Self::I64
                | Self::U8
                | Self::U16
                | Self::U32
                | Self::U64
        )
    }
    #[inline]
    fn is_float(&self) -> bool {
        matches!(self, Self::F32 | Self::F64)
    }
    fn ctype(&self, functions: &[FunctionInfo]) -> String {
        match self {
            Self::Unknown => "int64_t".into(),
            Self::Unit => "void".into(),
            Self::I8 => "int8_t".into(),
            Self::I16 => "int16_t".into(),
            Self::I32 => "int32_t".into(),
            Self::I64 => "int64_t".into(),
            Self::U8 => "uint8_t".into(),
            Self::U16 => "uint16_t".into(),
            Self::U32 => "uint32_t".into(),
            Self::U64 => "uint64_t".into(),
            Self::F32 => "float".into(),
            Self::F64 => "double".into(),
            Self::Str => "const char *".into(),
            Self::Ptr => "const void *".into(),
            Self::Adt(n) => format!("struct mak_{} *", mangle_ident(n)),
            Self::Function(id) => function_pointer_type(*id, functions),
            Self::ExternFunction { ctype, .. } => ctype.clone(),
        }
    }
}

#[inline]
fn rep_is_adt(r: &Rep) -> bool {
    matches!(r, Rep::Adt(_))
}

#[inline]
fn adt_struct_type(r: &Rep) -> String {
    match r {
        Rep::Adt(n) => format!("struct mak_{}", mangle_ident(n)),
        _ => "void".into(),
    }
}

#[derive(Clone, Debug)]
struct CtorInfo {
    id: usize,
    name: String,
    type_name: String,
    tag: String,
    args: Vec<Ty>,
    fields: Vec<Rep>,
    arity: usize,
}

#[derive(Clone, Debug)]
struct ExternalInfo {
    id: usize,
    decl: Extern,
    c_name: String,
    noreturn: bool,
}

#[derive(Clone, Debug)]
struct FunctionInfo {
    id: usize,
    c_name: String,
    source_name: Option<String>,
    expr: Expr,
    params: Vec<String>,
    captures: Vec<String>,
    param_reps: Vec<Rep>,
    capture_reps: Vec<Rep>,
    ret_rep: Rep,
    pure: bool,
}

#[derive(Clone, Debug)]
enum CallTarget {
    Function(usize),
    External(usize),
    Indirect(Box<LirExpr>),
}

#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
enum BinOpLir {
    Add,
    Sub,
    Mul,
    Div,
    Rem,
    And,
    Or,
    Xor,
    Shl,
    Shr,
    Eq,
    Ne,
    Lt,
    Gt,
    Le,
    Ge,
    LAnd,
    LOr,
}

#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
enum UnOpLir {
    Neg,
    Not,
    BitNot,
}

#[derive(Clone, Debug)]
enum LirPat {
    Wild,
    Bind { code: String, rep: Rep },
    Int(u64),
    Str(Vec<u8>),
    Ctor { ctor: usize, args: Vec<LirPat> },
    Tuple(Vec<LirPat>),
}

#[derive(Clone, Debug)]
enum LirExpr {
    Unit,
    Int(i128, Rep),
    Float(f64, Rep),
    Str(Vec<u8>),
    Var(String, Rep),
    FnRef(usize),
    Call {
        target: CallTarget,
        args: Vec<LirExpr>,
        rep: Rep,
        noreturn: bool,
        pure: bool,
    },
    Construct {
        ctor: usize,
        args: Vec<LirExpr>,
        rep: Rep,
    },
    Field {
        base: Box<LirExpr>,
        ctor: usize,
        index: usize,
        rep: Rep,
    },
    Let {
        code: String,
        rep: Rep,
        value: Box<LirExpr>,
        body: Box<LirExpr>,
    },
    Block {
        stmts: Vec<LirExpr>,
        tail: Box<LirExpr>,
    },
    Case {
        scrut: Box<LirExpr>,
        arms: Vec<LirArm>,
        rep: Rep,
    },
    Bin {
        op: BinOpLir,
        left: Box<LirExpr>,
        right: Box<LirExpr>,
        rep: Rep,
    },
    Un {
        op: UnOpLir,
        expr: Box<LirExpr>,
        rep: Rep,
    },
    RawAst(Box<Expr>, Rep, HashMap<String, Binding>),
    RuntimeCall {
        name: String,
        args: Vec<LirExpr>,
        rep: Rep,
    },
    RuntimeConstruct {
        name: String,
        args: Vec<LirExpr>,
        rep: Rep,
    },
    DirectC {
        code: String,
        rep: Rep,
    },
    Fallback,
}

#[derive(Clone, Debug)]
struct LirArm {
    pat: LirPat,
    body: LirExpr,
}

#[derive(Clone, Debug)]
struct LirFunction {
    info: FunctionInfo,
    body: LirExpr,
}

#[derive(Clone, Debug)]
struct PointerInfo {
    /// Representation of one indexed element.
    elem_rep: Rep,
    /// C spelling of that element type (without the trailing `*`).
    elem_ctype: String,
    /// If the element is itself a pointer, its pointed-to shape.
    nested: Option<Box<PointerInfo>>,
}

#[derive(Clone, Debug)]
enum Binding {
    Value {
        code: String,
        rep: Rep,
        /// Known pointed-to C type and recursively known pointer shape.  `Rep`
        /// intentionally stays coarse for ABI compatibility; this metadata is
        /// only used by indexing and pointer offsets.
        ptr_info: Option<PointerInfo>,
    },
    Function { id: usize },
}

#[derive(Clone, Debug)]
struct RecoveryNote {
    function: Option<usize>,
    span_lo: u32,
    span_hi: u32,
    stage: String,
    message: String,
}

// =========================================================================
// Runtime Plan: the feature graph the user asked for
// =========================================================================

#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Hash, PartialOrd, Ord)]
enum RtFeature {
    // Primitives (leaf features, no dependencies)
    Arena,
    Recovery,
    FloatBits,
    FoldEq,
    CanonEq,
    KindName,
    KindCompatible,
    Hash,

    // Dispatcher pieces
    FnDescType,
    CtorDescType,
    FnTable,
    CtorTable,
    FnDispatcher,
    CtorDispatcher,
}

#[derive(Clone, Debug, Default)]
struct RtPlan {
    features: BTreeSet<RtFeature>,
    runtime_fn_ids: BTreeSet<usize>,
    runtime_ctor_ids: BTreeSet<usize>,
}

impl RtPlan {
    fn on(&self, f: RtFeature) -> bool {
        self.features.contains(&f)
    }
    fn is_empty(&self) -> bool {
        self.features.is_empty()
    }
    fn any_dispatcher(&self) -> bool {
        self.on(RtFeature::FnDispatcher) || self.on(RtFeature::CtorDispatcher)
    }

    fn enable(&mut self, f: RtFeature) {
        self.features.insert(f);
    }

    fn detect(a: &Analyzer, fs: &[LirFunction], emitted_ids: &BTreeSet<usize>) -> Self {
        let mut p = RtPlan::default();
        p.enable(RtFeature::Recovery);

        let needs_arena = !a.constructors.is_empty();
        if needs_arena {
            p.enable(RtFeature::Arena);
        }

        fn walk(e: &LirExpr, p: &mut RtPlan, a: &Analyzer) {
            match e {
                LirExpr::RuntimeCall { args, .. } => {
                    let arity = args.len();
                    for id in a.runtime_function_candidates(arity) {
                        p.runtime_fn_ids.insert(id);
                    }
                    for arg in args {
                        walk(arg, p, a);
                    }
                }
                LirExpr::RuntimeConstruct { args, .. } => {
                    let arity = args.len();
                    for id in a.runtime_ctor_candidates(arity) {
                        p.runtime_ctor_ids.insert(id);
                    }
                    for arg in args {
                        walk(arg, p, a);
                    }
                }
                LirExpr::Call { target, args, .. } => {
                    if let CallTarget::Indirect(x) = target {
                        walk(x, p, a);
                    }
                    for arg in args {
                        walk(arg, p, a);
                    }
                }
                LirExpr::Construct { args, .. } => {
                    for arg in args {
                        walk(arg, p, a);
                    }
                }
                LirExpr::Field { base, .. } => walk(base, p, a),
                LirExpr::Let { value, body, .. } => {
                    walk(value, p, a);
                    walk(body, p, a);
                }
                LirExpr::Block { stmts, tail } => {
                    for s in stmts {
                        walk(s, p, a);
                    }
                    walk(tail, p, a);
                }
                LirExpr::Case { scrut, arms, .. } => {
                    walk(scrut, p, a);
                    for arm in arms {
                        walk(&arm.body, p, a);
                    }
                }
                LirExpr::Bin { left, right, .. } => {
                    walk(left, p, a);
                    walk(right, p, a);
                }
                LirExpr::Un { expr, .. } => walk(expr, p, a),
                LirExpr::RawAst(_, _, _) => {
                    p.enable(RtFeature::FnDispatcher);
                    p.enable(RtFeature::CtorDispatcher);
                }
                LirExpr::DirectC { .. }
                | LirExpr::Unit
                | LirExpr::Int(..)
                | LirExpr::Float(..)
                | LirExpr::Str(..)
                | LirExpr::Var(..)
                | LirExpr::FnRef(..)
                | LirExpr::Fallback => {}
            }
        }

        for f in fs {
            walk(&f.body, &mut p, a);
        }
        p.runtime_fn_ids.retain(|id| emitted_ids.contains(id));
        p.runtime_ctor_ids.retain(|id| id < &a.constructors.len());

        let has_fn = !p.runtime_fn_ids.is_empty();
        let has_ctor = !p.runtime_ctor_ids.is_empty();

        if has_fn || has_ctor || p.on(RtFeature::FnDispatcher) || p.on(RtFeature::CtorDispatcher) {
            if has_fn {
                p.enable(RtFeature::FnDispatcher);
            }
            if has_ctor {
                p.enable(RtFeature::CtorDispatcher);
            }
            if p.on(RtFeature::FnDispatcher) || p.on(RtFeature::CtorDispatcher) {
                p.enable(RtFeature::FloatBits);
                p.enable(RtFeature::FoldEq);
                p.enable(RtFeature::CanonEq);
                p.enable(RtFeature::KindName);
                p.enable(RtFeature::KindCompatible);
                p.enable(RtFeature::Hash);
            }
            if has_fn {
                p.enable(RtFeature::FnDescType);
                p.enable(RtFeature::FnTable);
            }
            if has_ctor {
                p.enable(RtFeature::CtorDescType);
                p.enable(RtFeature::CtorTable);
            }
        }

        if p.on(RtFeature::Arena)
            || p.on(RtFeature::FnDispatcher)
            || p.on(RtFeature::CtorDispatcher)
        {
            p.enable(RtFeature::Recovery);
        }

        p
    }
}


// ============================================================================
// Whole-program memory analysis
// ============================================================================

#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
enum MemorySiteKind {
    Construct,
    CallResult,
    DynamicCallResult,
    CaseResult,
    RuntimeCall,
    RuntimeConstruct,
    DirectAst,
}

#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
enum MemoryStrategy {
    Stack,
    Arena,
    Heap,
}

#[derive(Clone, Debug, Default)]
struct MemoryPlan {
    rounds: usize,
    converged: bool,
    ast_nodes: usize,
    lir_nodes: usize,
    allocation_sites: usize,
    stack_sites: usize,
    arena_sites: usize,
    heap_sites: usize,
    estimated_stack_bytes: usize,
    estimated_arena_bytes: usize,
    estimated_heap_bytes: usize,
    stack_safe_functions: HashSet<usize>,
    force_heap_unknown: bool,
}

impl MemoryPlan {
    const MAX_ROUNDS: usize = 4096;
    const STACK_OBJECT_LIMIT: usize = 16 * 1024;
    const ARENA_OBJECT_LIMIT: usize = 256 * 1024;

    fn analyze(a: &Analyzer, p: &Program, fs: &[LirFunction]) -> Self {
        let mut plan = Self::default();
        plan.ast_nodes = memory_ast_nodes(p);

        // The fixed-point state intentionally tracks only properties that can
        // change while call edges are propagated.  This keeps thousands of
        // iterations cheap and deterministic instead of doing thousands of
        // whole expensive AST passes.
        let mut dynamic = HashMap::<usize, bool>::new();
        for f in fs {
            dynamic.insert(f.info.id, memory_lir_has_dynamic(&f.body));
        }

        let mut converged = false;
        for round in 1..=Self::MAX_ROUNDS {
            let mut changed = false;
            for f in fs {
                let mut next = *dynamic.get(&f.info.id).unwrap_or(&false);
                let mut callees = Vec::new();
                memory_collect_function_edges(&f.body, &mut callees);
                for callee in callees {
                    if let Some(x) = dynamic.get(&callee).copied() {
                        next |= x;
                    }
                }
                if next && !dynamic.get(&f.info.id).copied().unwrap_or(false) {
                    dynamic.insert(f.info.id, true);
                    changed = true;
                }
            }
            plan.rounds = round;
            if !changed {
                converged = true;
                break;
            }
        }
        plan.converged = converged;
        plan.force_heap_unknown = !converged;

        for f in fs {
            // A stack allocation is safe across ordinary recursive calls because
            // each invocation receives its own frame. Tail recursion lowered to
            // a loop is the important exception: alloca inside that loop would
            // monotonically grow the frame, so those functions use pooled or
            // dynamic storage instead.
            if !lir_has_self_tail_call(&f.body, f.info.id) {
                plan.stack_safe_functions.insert(f.info.id);
            }
        }

        for f in fs {
            plan.lir_nodes += memory_lir_nodes(&f.body);
            let mut sites = Vec::<(MemorySiteKind, Rep, Option<usize>)>::new();
            memory_collect_alloc_sites(&f.body, Some(f.info.id), &mut sites);
            for (kind, rep, fid) in sites {
                plan.account_site(a, kind, &rep, fid);
            }
        }

        // The entry body can be emitted outside a named function.
        if !fs.iter().any(|f| matches!(f.info.source_name.as_deref(), Some("main"))) {
            let mut lowerer = Lowerer::new(a);
            let mut env = HashMap::new();
            let entry = find_structural_entry(p, &a.function_by_source);
            if entry.is_none() {
                let body = lowerer.lower_expr(&p.body, &mut env, None);
                plan.lir_nodes += memory_lir_nodes(&body);
                let mut sites = Vec::<(MemorySiteKind, Rep, Option<usize>)>::new();
                memory_collect_alloc_sites(&body, None, &mut sites);
                for (kind, rep, fid) in sites {
                    plan.account_site(a, kind, &rep, fid);
                }
            }
        }

        plan
    }

    fn account_site(&mut self, a: &Analyzer, kind: MemorySiteKind, rep: &Rep, fid: Option<usize>) {
        if !rep_is_adt(rep) {
            return;
        }
        let (bytes, _align) = adt_object_layout(a, match rep {
            Rep::Adt(n) => n,
            _ => return,
        });
        let strategy = self.strategy(kind, fid, bytes);
        self.allocation_sites += 1;
        match strategy {
            MemoryStrategy::Stack => {
                self.stack_sites += 1;
                self.estimated_stack_bytes = self.estimated_stack_bytes.saturating_add(bytes);
            }
            MemoryStrategy::Arena => {
                self.arena_sites += 1;
                self.estimated_arena_bytes = self.estimated_arena_bytes.saturating_add(bytes);
            }
            MemoryStrategy::Heap => {
                self.heap_sites += 1;
                self.estimated_heap_bytes = self.estimated_heap_bytes.saturating_add(bytes);
            }
        }
    }

    fn strategy(&self, kind: MemorySiteKind, fid: Option<usize>, bytes: usize) -> MemoryStrategy {
        // Runtime resolution is intrinsically a dynamic boundary. We use heap
        // storage there. Source-level ADT values go through the arena below until
        // a per-site escape proof is available; simply being inside a
        // non-tail-recursive function is not enough to make stack storage safe.
        if matches!(
            kind,
            MemorySiteKind::RuntimeCall
                | MemorySiteKind::RuntimeConstruct
                | MemorySiteKind::DynamicCallResult
                | MemorySiteKind::DirectAst
        ) {
            return MemoryStrategy::Heap;
        }

        if self.force_heap_unknown {
            return MemoryStrategy::Heap;
        }

        if bytes > Self::ARENA_OBJECT_LIMIT {
            return MemoryStrategy::Heap;
        }
